--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The mission is **aggregation**: minimize the number of robots outside the target region (the BLACK-floored strip bordered by BLUE walls).

Key observations:
- The target region has BLACK floor and BLUE walls
- The other strip also has BLACK floor but GREEN walls
- The middle area has GRAY floor
- The champion uses `GoAwayColor` with color 2 (green) — this makes robots move away from the GREEN walls, which pushes them toward the BLUE side

The champion scores 17002.39 with a single state: GoAwayColor (green). This is a reasonable baseline but not optimal since robots need to:
1. Navigate to the BLUE-walled strip
2. Stay there

Better strategies:
- GoToColor with color 3 (blue) to directly approach blue walls
- Use floor color (BLACK=0) to detect when in target strip, then stop or aggregate
- Combine: explore/go toward blue walls, then stop when on black floor

Let me think about what colors are available:
- clr 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan

The target strip has BLUE walls → GoToColor with clr=3 (blue) should work well.

**Strategy:**
1. **Survival**: Keep champion exactly
2. **Exploitation**: Variations of GoAwayColor(green) and GoToColor(blue)
3. **Exploration**: Multi-state FSMs that detect floor color to stop in black strip, or combine behaviors

For multi-state machines:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → if on BLACK floor → transition to Stop/Attraction
- State 1: Stop/Attraction → if on GRAY floor → go back to GoToColor(blue)

FSM designs:

1. Champion (exact copy)
2. GoToColor blue (clr=3)
3. GoToColor blue + stop on black
4. GoAwayColor green + attraction on black
5. Two-state: GoToColor(blue) ↔ Stop with floor conditions
6. Two-state: Exploration → GoToColor(blue) on black
7. Three-state with exploration, GoToColor, attraction
8. GoAwayColor green with different LED
9. Attraction behavior
10. FlockCenter
11. Two-state: GoToColor(blue) → Attraction when on black
12. Three-state machine
13. GoToColor blue + neighbor conditions
14. ZigZag exploration → GoToColor blue

## Reasoning

The champion scores 3220.49 with a 3-state FSM:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → if BLACK floor (p=0.9) → State 1 (Attraction); if sees GREEN (p=0.8) → State 2 (GoAwayColor green)

- State 2: GoAwayColor(green) → if sees BLUE (p=0.8) → State 0 (GoToColor blue)

This is a very clever structure: navigate toward blue walls, aggregate on black floor, flee from green walls.

2. **Exploitation**: Small tweaks to probabilities, gains, thresholds

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The new champion scores 2629.07. The key change: State 2 is now **Attraction(4)** instead of GoAwayColor(green)! The structure:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → BLACK floor (p=0.95) → State 1 (FlockCenter 50); sees GREEN (p=0.85) → State 2 (Attraction 4)
- State 1: FlockCenter(50) → GRAY floor (p=0.95) → State 0
- State 2: Attraction(4) → sees BLUE (p=0.85) → State 0

So when robots see green walls (wrong side), they attract to neighbors (which helps group them) then when they see blue they navigate toward it. This is clever.

**Exploitation ideas:**
- Vary Attraction gain in State 2 (3, 5)
- Vary FlockCenter gain (40, 60, 70)
- Vary probabilities (0.98/0.9, 0.99/0.9)
- Add second transition from State 2 (e.g., on BLACK floor → State 1)
- Add second transition from State 1 (sees blue → GoToColor)
- Try att2=5 and att2=3

**Exploration ideas:**
- 4-state: add GoAwayColor(green) as explicit escape state
- Try making State 2 FlockCenter too
- Try different trigger for State 2 (BLACK floor instead of sees green)
- Add neighbor-count transitions

## Reasoning

The new champion scores 2355.82 with att2=3 (not 4!). So the structure remains:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → BLACK floor (p=0.95) → FlockCenter; sees GREEN (p=0.85) → Attraction
- State 1: FlockCenter(50) → GRAY floor (p=0.95) → GoToColor(blue)
- State 2: Attraction(3) → sees BLUE (p=0.85) → GoToColor(blue)

att2=3 beats att2=4. Let me try att2=2 and att2=1 as well.

The key question now: can we improve by:
1. Tuning fc1 (try 30, 40, 60, 80)
2. Tuning att2 (try 1, 2)
3. Tuning probabilities further (0.98, 0.99 for p0x0/p1x0; 0.9, 0.95 for p0x1/p2x0)
4. Adding transitions to existing states
5. Trying a 4-state machine with an additional behavior

The trend: att=3 < att=4 < att=5, so lower attraction in state 2 might be better. Also fc=50 has been consistent. Let me also try having State 2 transition to State 1 directly when on BLACK floor (instead of back to GoToColor).

**Exploitation:**
- att2=1, att2=2 variants
- fc1=30, fc1=40, fc1=60 with att2=3
- p variations: 0.98/0.9, 0.99/0.95

**Exploration:**
- 4-state with Stop when clustered
- State 2 transitions to State 1 on black floor
- Add neighbor transition from State 1
- Try GoAwayColor(green) as state 2 with current best probabilities but att2=3 structure elsewhere

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion hasn't improved since round 6 (2355.82). The same 3-state FSM keeps winning. We're stuck at a local optimum. Time for more aggressive exploration while still exploiting the champion.

The champion structure is solid: GoToColor(blue) → FlockCenter on black → Attraction(3) when sees green → back to GoToColor when sees blue.

**What hasn't been tried much:**
- Varying the transition probability for the GRAY floor condition in State 1 (always been 0.95)
- Trying FlockCenter with lower gain in State 1 (20, 10)
- Making State 2 transition to State 1 directly (on black floor) rather than back to State 0
- Using a 4-state machine where we have GoToColor → FlockCenter → Attraction → GoAwayColor(green) as a cycle
- Trying different floor conditions: what if State 1 transitions on BLACK floor (stay in strip) instead of GRAY?
- What if State 2 uses GoAwayColor(green) AND attraction?
- Trying att2=3 but with sees-green triggering differently

**Key insight to explore:** What if State 1 (FlockCenter) also had a transition triggered by seeing GREEN walls (to escape to Attraction state)? Or what if the Attraction state had a transition to FlockCenter when on BLACK floor?

Let me also try: State 2 → State 1 (FlockCenter) when on BLACK floor, which would mean robots that are already on the right side go directly to flock rather than back through GoToColor.

- fc1 values: 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 90, 100 — 50 keeps winning
- att2 values: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 — 3 keeps winning
- Probabilities: 0.8-0.99 range explored
- Extra transitions added to various states — haven't helped

**New exploration directions:**
1. What if we swap the floor color triggers? Use WHITE instead of GRAY (though arena may not have white)
2. What if State 0 has a BLACK floor trigger going to State 2 (Attraction) instead of State 1 (FlockCenter)? Maybe attraction first, then flock?
3. What if we use a completely different state for "wrong strip" — e.g., GoToColor(blue) from state 2 directly?
4. What if State 1 uses Stop instead of FlockCenter?
5. Try a 2-state machine: GoToColor(blue) with BLACK→Attraction, and Attraction with GRAY→GoToColor
6. Try GoAwayColor(green) as State 0 instead of GoToColor(blue)
7. Try having State 0 start as Attraction, then GoToColor when on gray
8. What if the color perception in State 0 triggers on BLUE (clr=3) instead of GREEN (clr=2)?
9. Try FlockCenter as the "wrong strip" recovery behavior
10. Try a completely different topology: 3 states but different flow

Let me try some fundamentally different topologies and also fine-tune around the champion more carefully.

## Reasoning

We're in the final round. The champion has been stuck at 2355.82 for several rounds. I need to fix the error from last round: condition C=3 (neighbours ABOVE threshold) uses `--p` as an INTEGER neighbour count, not a probability. I mistakenly used `--c0x1 3` with `--p0x1 0.85` - condition 3 needs an integer count.

Let me focus on:
1. **Survival**: Keep champion exactly
2. **Exploitation**: Fine-tune the champion's parameters carefully
3. **Exploration**: Try genuinely different topologies that haven't been tried

- fc1 values: 1, 5, 15, 25 (lower end not fully explored)
- att2 values: try combining att with a second transition

- Try a completely different 3-state topology: GoAwayColor(green) → FlockCenter → GoToColor(blue)
- Try 4-state with careful construction
- Try Stop as state 1 (robots stop when on black floor - simple aggregation)
- Try different condition types for transitions (neighbor count C=3, C=4)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
